## Document 002: 10月美国非农数据点评：新增非农大超预期，12月加息预期强化

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月美国非农数据点评：新增非农大超预期，12月加息预期强化.pdf
Report source: 交银国际证券
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

此报告最后部分的分析师披露、商业关系披露和免责声明为报告的一部分，必须阅读。
下载本公司之研究报告，可从彭博搜寻 NH BCM  或 登录研究部网站 https://research.bocomgroup.com 
交银国际研究 
宏观策略

2026 年 11 月 11 日
全球宏观 
新增非农大超预期，12 月加息预期强化——10 月美国非农数据点评
 10 月非农数据显著高于市场预期。美国 10 月新增非农就业 16.5 万，市场
的预期增加 7万。前两月的数据也都上修，9月数据首次修正上修 3.1万人
到 6.0万人，8月数据第二次修正上修至 18.6万人，相对于初值上修了 2.4
万人。
 非季调的新增非农业显示就业增长强于去年。非季调非农就业 10 月增加
了 102.3 万人，去年 10 月仅增加 62.6 万人。去年 10 月季调后非农就业下
降 14万人。我们在上月数据点评中，也强调季调会引入噪音，虽然 9月季
调后的新增非农偏弱，但是非季调今年的新增非农强于去年。
 失业率小幅回落，劳动力市场整体仍不弱。失业率 10月小幅下降到 4.1%，
上月 4.2%。从周期的角度看，目前的失业率水平仍然处于较低的水平，
离任何衰退时期的水平还较远。劳动参与率由 61.8%进一步升至 61.9%，
失业人口减少约 4.1 万人，因此失业率下降即来自分子的下降，也来自分
母的增长。
 10 月美国工资压力略有增加，尽管平均时薪同比增速小幅回落。10 月美
国私营非农企业全体雇员平均时薪环比上涨 0.4%，高于市场预期，同比
涨幅为 3.1%，略低于 9月修订后同比的 3.2%，9月初值为 3.0%。平均每周
工时维持在 34.4 小时。
 10 月高频数据显示失业仍处于较低水平。10 月 31 日的当周初次申领失业
金人数为 18.9万人，略低于上一周，近 4周平均 19.5万人，仍处于较低水
平。当前，美国就业市场处于“低招聘、低辞职”的状态，招聘率和辞职
率都不高。
 12 月美联储加息预期进一步增强。在强劲就业与工资数据影响下，市场
对 12 月美联储加息 25 个基点的概率由公布前约七成升至接近八成。我们
认为，12 月继续加息仍是基准情形，其后的美联储政策路径仍需结合通
胀与就业质量进一步判断。
 市场反应：美债利率小幅上行，美元指数上涨。数据公布当日，美股标普
收涨 0.3%，纳斯达克上涨 1.1%，道琼斯指数下跌 0.5%。分行业看，标普
500 行业指数中信息技术和通信服务当日涨幅靠前，前一日科技板块下跌
较多或是重要背景因素；而必需消费、金融、房地产下跌较多。美国国债
利率普遍小幅上行，10 年期国债利率上升 2 个基点，1 年期国债利率上升
5 个基点，3 个月国债利率上升 2 个基点。美元指数当日上涨 0.7%。
王博群, PhD, FRM
hugh.wang@bocomgroup.com
(852) 3766 1853

### Page 8

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。
香港市场的标杆指数为恒生综合指数，A股市场的标杆指数
为MSCI中国A股指数，美国上市中概股的标杆指数为标普美
国中概股50（美元）指数

信托的受托人，而分析员、其配偶、其亲生或领养的未成年子女或其未成年继子女是该信托的受益人或酌情对象；或iii）惯于或有义务按

Greater China Technology Semiconductors

Greater China Semiconductors  | Asia Pacific
Cloud Semis:  Peak Growth, Less
Upside
We turn cautious on cloud semis as our US team expects ~20%

of 2028 US data center capacity additions could shift to 2029,
potentially impacting cloud semis suppliers' 2028 earnings.
What’s Changed
Montage Technology Co Ltd (6809.HK) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target HK$432.00 HK$207.00
Montage Technology Co Ltd (688008.SS)
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target Rmb377.00 Rmb180.00
Aspeed Technology (5274.TWO)
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$24,000.00 NT$14,417.00
2028 capex under the spotlight. We turned neutral on cloud semi suppliers in

September 2024 on capex/revision peaking risks and GB200 execution concerns,
then upgraded Aspeed in June 2025 as revisions approached a trough and rack-level
execution improved (Cloud semis: time to turn constructive). AI agents reinforced
our conviction in inference demand.  In our recent note, Cloud Semis: Growing Cloud
Capex into 2027, we remained constructive  on our Cloud Semis coverage due to
ongoing hyperscaler capex growth.  However, after the US midterms, we are mindful

of delays in project approvals and initiation, and existing labor and material
constraints, in terms of what that could mean for hyperscalers' 2028 capex, and the
build-out and capex cycle.   Therefore, we see rising risks  over the next 2-4 quarters
for our coverage; earnings growth should provide support, but further multiple
expansion may become more difficult, as the direction of growth matters more,
even as absolute growth remains strong.
Cut Montage & Aspeed to EW. From our Asia supply-chain perspective, peaking

revision breadth and greater uncertainty on 2027/28 general-server production
warrants more conservative forecasts. Aspeed: we continue to like the stock from a
longer-term perspective given its strong supply chain positioning but see limited
rerating potential. Montage: we see potentially slower China procurement and
derating risk; structural content growth remains supportive, but exposure reduction
is preferable before deceleration. Upside risks include a resumption of earnings

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Revisions look mature: Earnings-revision breadth is peaking; Asia supply-chain
visibility on 2027/28 general-server production is weakening.
Reduce exposure: Lower Montage estimates; downgrade Aspeed/Montage to EW.
November 9, 2026 04:00 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
The cloud capex cycle is being impacted by the US midterms: Our US team now expects a ~20% of 2028 US data center capacity
additions to shift to 2029,  including some projects in Texas and Ohio, as governors introduce new data center legislation (Buy the Drop: AI
Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms by Stephen Byrd). Stephen has shifted ~5GW of US data center capacity additions from
2028 to 2029  ( Exhibit 1 ) (equivalent to 20% of US and 10% of global 2028 capacity additions). This implies US data center GW additions

decline 4% Y/Y in 2028 before rising 38% Y/Y in 2029, while global data center additions increase 9% and    20% Y/Y in 2028 and 2029.
Our US team believes this could create an air pocket across the tech supply chain in 2028, potentially reducing pricing power and leading
to a inventory build. Despite potential project delays, our US team maintains its  hyperscaler capex forecasts (Buy the Drop: AI Investment
Opportunities Post the US Midterms) at US$1.6trn/US$1.8trn in 2027/28 ( Exhibit 4 ), as it  expects the hyperscalers to spend and build as

much capacity as they can, while attempting to adjust their build locations to other states that are more data center friendly (like
Tennessee, Louisiana, and others).

Aspeed: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target (base case scenario value) from   NT$24,000 to NT$14,417: This
primarily reflects lower growth assumptions, alongside earnings revisions. We reduce our
terminal growth rate from 5.5% to 3.0% to better align with macro growth outlook, and
reduce our intermediate growth rate from 21.4% to 18.0% to reflect a potential slowdown
in market demand.   Other key assumptions are unchanged, including:

• Cash payout ratio of 85%
• Cost of equity of 9.8% (2% risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.3 beta)
Our bull and bear case scenario values  also decrease from NT$29,300 and NT$11,500, to
NT$17,600 and NT$6,900, respectively, implying 51x/20x our 2027e EPS estimates.

MS Rating
▪ Cloud capex Y/Y growth could peak in

1Q27, and Asia supply-chain order visibility is
weakening.
▪ Aspeed's shipments are currently capped
by T-glass shortage, but the company is now
qualifying E-glass, which we believe can help
expand its shipments to over 2mn units per
month.
▪ Aspeed maintains the lion's share in ASIC
racks. Google TPU has long adopted MCU,
while we see it utilizing Aspeed's BMC in its
next-generation TPU. We expect this to
contribute 13% of revenue in 2027.
▪ Our price target implies 42x our 2027e

EPS, in line with the 42x average one-year
forward P/E since 2020.

Montage: Valuation Methodology
We cut our A-share price target to Rmb180 from Rmb377: The change reflects our
lower earnings estimates. Our price target is our base case value, derived from a residual
income (RI) model, which we believe best captures the company's sustainable long-term
growth.  We also cut our intermediate growth rate estimate from 19.3% to 13.6% to reflect
weaker downstream demand estimates. Other RI model assumptions are unchanged,

including a 8.4% cost of equity (beta 1.2, risk-free rate 3.0%, and risk premium 4.5%), a
payout ratio of 30%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%. We apply an updated exchange
rate of HK$1.15 : Rmb1, assuming no H-share premium or discount to the A-share, to derive
our H-share price target, which we lower to HK$207 from HK$432.
For the A-share, we cut our bull case value to Rmb218 from Rmb472, and our bear case
value to Rmb83 from Rmb171. For the H-share, we cut our bull case value to HK$259 from

HK$540, and our bear case value to HK$94 from HK$196. These revisions are in line with
our base case value changes.

Risk Reward - Montage Technology Co Ltd (6809.HK)
HK$207.00
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions:
8.4% CoE (1.2 beta, 3.0% risk-free rate, and 4.5% risk premium)
30% payout ratio
13.6% medium-term growth rate
4% terminal growth rate
These assumptions reﬂect cloud capex growth, DRAM interface technology migration, and
Montage's strong position in China's datacenter semi localization. We then apply an exchange
rate of HK$1.15 HKD:Rmb1, assuming no H-share discount to the A-share.

Morgan Stanley EstimatesMean
Consensus Price Target Distribution HK$207.00 HK$539.35
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ Montage is China’s only pure datacenter
player, with the majority of its revenue
generated from interconnect chips, mainly
memory interconnect chips for server
DRAM.
▪ Core DRAM interconnect business is
levered to the global cloud capex/memory
super cycle.
▪ New AI business including MRCD/MDB,
PCIe retimers, and PCIe switch is beneﬁting

from AI server demand.
▪ China semi localization is driving market
share gains, especially for PCIe products.
▪ However, we note a potential slower
China procurement and derating risk.
▪ Our PT implies 37x 2027e P/E, vs the A-
share historical average of 70x since 2020.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

We hosted NYPCB’s 3Q26 post result on November 9th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) NYPCB mentioned its 4Q26 GM on ABF substrate already 
exceeds 40% (vs. ~15% in 2025 and 25% in 1H26) driven mainly by 
the solid high-end product demand (which contributed 50%+ of the 
ABF revenue in 2026 vs. <20% in 2025), while the company 
mentioned it will gain more high-end market share in 2027 which 
should further drive company’s overall GM level.  
(2) For the new high-end projects in 2027, NYPCB mentioned it will 
gain significant share in the key US CSP next-gen AI project, and 
high-end server CPU project. Also, the company mentioned it 
expects a proactive adoption on 1.6T Ethernet switch demand 
driven by CSPs investment in AI infrastructure, driving its high-end 
switch substrate ASP (the upgrade on substrate, from 800G to 1.6T, 
suggesting a 50%+ higher substrate unit price, per GSe).  
(3) In terms of the ABF substrate pricing, NYPCB believes its ABF 
substrate ASP will further go up by 10-15% QoQ in 4Q26, while it 
also expects the pricing hike ratio to be at least maintained at this 
level from 1Q27-4Q27, without seeing much downside risk. 
Moreover, the company believes the ASP growth could be even 
faster than its expectation if the high-end orders accelerate in 
coming quarters or if they see an even tigher S/D outlook for the 
overall ABF substrate industry.  
(4) In terms of the BT substrate pricing outlook, the company 
mentioned the pricing hike ratio will be low single digits % QoQ in 
4Q26, which will further trend up in 1Q27-4Q27, with a high 
confidence level on the shortage of BT substrate in coming quarters.  
(4) In terms of 4Q26 revenue guidance, the company is expecting 
~20% QoQ revenue growth, driven mainly by the high-end ABF 
projects ramp up + new AI project ramps + BT substrate pricing hike

### Page 3

(low single digits % QoQ); moreover, the company expects its 1Q27 revenue to be flat 
QoQ, despite the ~20% less working days. For GM, the company expects 4Q26 GM to be 
low to mid 30% which should further improve in 1Q27.  
(5) The company mentioned it is working on a new capacity investment plan for 2028, 
but has not yet finalized the plan. In terms of the funding method, NYPCB mentioned it 
will not do any equity based fundraising, but will rely on internal cash or bank loan.  
(6) NYPCB also mentioned it will focus only on CoPoS technology, whose substrate 
pricing is also US$1000+ (vs. US$100-200 for the most high end ABF substrate now), 
while the product demand could start ramping up from 2028 and start contributing 
significant revenue from 2029. For EMIB-T, NYPCB believes the penetration rate could 
only be 10% in the foreseeable future, and it has no plan to invest in this small market.  
We maintain our positive view on the overall ABF substrate industry, with 34%/51% 
shortage ratio in 2027/28 (see here), and believe such high shortage ratio will protect 
companies’ GM/OPM even if we start to see some slowdown on AI CAPEX in coming 
years. We started to see all ABF substrate players’ GM surpass 40% in 4Q26, which is 
much faster than our original expectation (Taiwan ABF substrate suppliers reach 40%+ 
GM at the 6-7th quarters of the shortage in last upcycle, but it only takes 3-4 quarters 
for Taiwan players to hit the 40%+ GM, driven mainly by the solid AI upgrading demand 
this time). We expect NYPCB’s ABF substrate GM could reach historical peak level 
(55-60%) in 2H27, suggesting a fruitful upside for the EPS growth in coming years (we 
expect NYPCB’s EPS to grow by 150%+ 2025-29E CAGR), and believe the ABF substrate 
will continue to be a key bottleneck of the overall semi/tech supply chain in the next 3-5 
years.  
Maintain Buy on NYPCB, as the key spot price ABF substrate supplier in the market 
which should benefit strongly from the S/D shortage and pricing hike in the 
foreseeable future, with an unchanged TP of NT$2,700 (based on 21x 2028E EPS).  
Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s

Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB

Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아쉬웠습니다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추정치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 YoY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발생한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기여했던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불가피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만회할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아반떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상대적으로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증가와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영업이익 추정치는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감익에서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상반기까지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가능성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차종에서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부정적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적용한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목표주가를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성과와 RA 설립, 12월 모셔널 자율주행 상용화와 웨이모向 로보택시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사업에서의 가시성도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 227: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0.pdf

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q 부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520 억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1 조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV 에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

## Document 239: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름삼성바이오로직스(207940) 환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름환율 하락에도 견조한 흐름 - 기업.pdf

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

### Page 4

4

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1810 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 294: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

## Document 326: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key risks.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Low volatility masks a wide range of tail risks for 2027. AI stress,
another inﬂation shock, ﬁscal concerns, elections and
geopolitical escalation could all trigger sharp FX moves, while
ﬁnancial repression and a carry unwind add further asymmetric
risks

Chris Turner and Francesco Pesole
AI bust: expect unorthodox FX reactions
The resilience of the AI trade in equities has been tested of late, and
a more decisive unwind could become an overarching FX driver next
year. We think the impact of a major US tech selloﬀ on currencies
would depend on how far it spreads to broader US and global risk
assets, whether it heavily threatens ﬁnancial stability, and how it
impacts the inﬂation backdrop. Crucially, the initial FX reaction may
look very diﬀerent from the medium-term move.
The dollar still retains decent safe-haven appeal by our estimates,
having recovered from the 2025 “sell America” period. Yet episodes
in 2026 where AI concerns hit tech stocks without materially

### Page 2

spilling into broader equities were accompanied by some USD
underperformance. We think contained US tech-speciﬁc weakness
would continue to weigh moderately on the dollar next year, with
low-yielding haven currencies such as the yen, Swiss franc and euro
beneﬁting the most.
The dynamics would be diﬀerent if tech losses triggered a broader
selloﬀ. In that scenario, we would expect an initial safe-haven boost
to the dollar, followed by a larger depreciation as the shock

deepens and the Federal Reserve response comes into focus. The
greater the threat to ﬁnancial stability, the stronger the case for the
Fed to provide liquidity and prevent a global ﬁnancial crisis-style
credit crunch, ultimately weighing on the dollar. Over the medium
term, we would expect JPY, CHF and EUR to outperform, while the
Australian dollar, New Zealand dollar and non-European EM
currencies would be hit harder, consistent with recent AI-driven

selloﬀs (see chart below).
There is, however, an important inﬂation caveat. A severe risk-oﬀ
shock would itself reduce inﬂation risks through potential job
losses, negative wealth eﬀects and tighter credit conditions. But if
inﬂation remains elevated, the Fed may be slower to ease, not least
to preserve the safe-haven role of the Treasury market. In that
scenario, we think the dollar would outperform for longer, while
European currencies would be less insulated than under a more

benign inﬂation backdrop.

currencies

### Page 3

Asian currencies have been excluded due to time-zone mismatch
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
A new 2022 for inﬂation
Our baseline assumes contained second-round eﬀects from the
energy shock, allowing major European central banks to cut rates
towards the end of 2027. Four risks could challenge that view.
Second-round eﬀects may simply take longer to emerge, as a
prolonged crisis and higher gas prices erode corporate buﬀers and
feed into food and services inﬂation. Tighter labour markets could

amplify the shock through stronger wage growth, while renewed
Middle East escalation could drive another sharp rise in energy
prices. Finally, greater ﬁscal support could cushion the hit to
growth, but at the cost of higher medium-term inﬂation pressures.
In such a scenario, we would expect broad dollar gains. Historically,
low-yielders have tended to suﬀer most when Fed tightening drives
global yields higher, but we would be wary of applying that

playbook mechanically in 2027. The relative central bank response
should be the key diﬀerentiator, with those that have tightened less
so far retaining more ammunition to defend their currencies.
We still see the Norwegian krone and Swedish krona as particularly
vulnerable given their low liquidity and high beta, while AUD could
be a standout loser given the Reserve Bank of Australia's limited
room to tighten further amid domestic vulnerabilities. EUR and JPY,

by contrast, could prove more resilient than in 2022, provided the

Current central bank rates vs latest peak
Source: ING, Macrobond
Fiscal takeover: contained, but
potentially explosive risk
We’ve all seen the French bond sell-oﬀ unfold over recent
months. The heart of the story is one of budget credibility. If
investors suspect that a government will not or cannot amend a
worrying budget trajectory, then they will demand higher

coupons or, in extremis, as in Greece 2010, refuse to buy
government debt. France is no Greece and our base case
assumes that some modest budget consolidation can keep
French spreads contained in 2027. Even if French politicians fail
to address investor concerns, the ECB’s Transmission Protection
Instrument (TPI) backstop can prevent broader contagion.
What about the US? One area of concern is that near 60% of US
Treasury issuance is in the form of short-term bill issuance, as

well as its large reﬁnancing requirements. What happens if
inﬂation spikes and the US Treasury has to pay ever-higher
reﬁnancing rates? Equally, what happens if White House policy
again upends the dollar-US Treasury safe-haven correlation as
was the case in March/April 2025? As we saw at the time, the
breakdown in these correlations can trigger some of the most

extreme dollar selling. And the fact that investors are again
running low FX hedge ratios on US assets warns of a similar
setup to early 2025.
Debt ﬁnancing overview
Source: IMF, ING
Watch the EU election calendar!
2027 will be a busy year for eurozone political risk, with markets
focused primarily on ﬁscal implications and the threat of EU
political fragmentation. Expect any negative EUR impact to make
the Swiss franc a standout outperformer, thanks to its European

safe-haven role.
French elections combine both ﬁscal and EU-wide risks and are
likely to matter most. The biggest downside for the euro would
come from a surprise victory by Jean-Luc Mélenchon in the April-
May presidential election or a lack of a clear majority in potential
parliamentary elections over the summer. To a lesser extent, a
Marine Le Pen victory could also weigh on sentiment if early signals
point to a more confrontational approach towards the EU than

already expected.
In Germany, several state elections will be watched for signs of
greater government instability or further gains by the Eurosceptic
Alternative for Germany (AfD). Markets will focus particularly on the
implications for Germany’s reform agenda, and by extension the
growth outlook.

### Page 6

Italian elections rank third among our political risks. Although
scheduled by December, a May-June vote looks increasingly likely.
Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni remains favoured to secure another
mandate, potentially backed by a broader coalition. Italian asset
resilience has relied on Meloni’s ﬁscal discipline, so a surprise
victory by the centre-left bloc would, in our view, pose material
downside risks to BTPs and the euro.
A busy election calendar for next year

Source: ING, average of pollsters
Debasement trade strikes back
2025 was the year of dollar debasement, when fears over a
captured Fed led to pressure on the dollar against the Swiss franc,
while bitcoin and gold went bid. We had a ﬂash of that trade again
this August, when Scott Bessent’s extension of longer-dated US
Treasury buybacks unnerved the bond market and USD/CHF was hit
hard.
Were US Treasury yields to rise sharply next year, the theme of

‘ﬁnancial repression’ could re-emerge. Here the US Treasury could
try to contain Treasury yields by providing regulatory incentives to
banks to hold more Treasuries or put pressure on the Fed to
intervene. Any suggestion that the Treasury would not be allowing

USD is an efﬁcient safe-haven, but things
can change
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The end of the carry trade?
Popular carry trade target currencies through 2026 have again
been the Turkish lira and the Nigerian naira. On the former, Turkish
presidential and parliamentary elections must be held by May
2028 and present some risk that the Central Bank of Turkey may

opt to change its policy of a controlled depreciation in the lira. That
would come as a big shock to the market. Nigeria looks slightly
better positioned after recent reforms to fuel subsidies and in the
energy sector.
When looking at the popular funding currencies of the yen, we
doubt slightly more aggressive BoJ tightening would be enough to
trigger a big short squeeze in the yen. And speculators are actually
running net long yen positions today as opposed to the large short

yen positions held in summer 2024 – the last time we had a major
adjustment in yen positioning.
The biggest driver of a carry trade unwind would probably come
through a ﬁnancial shock. Were any unwind in the AI trade to
uncover stresses or imbalances in, for example, the private credit
area and lead to ﬁnancial distress, volatility would surge and value-

### Page 8

at-risk considerations could prompt indiscriminate deleveraging.
We haven’t really seen much ﬁnancial distress since the Silicon
Valley Bank crisis of 2023.
A big carry trade unwind would be yen and Swiss franc positive,
while negative for the high-yielding activity currencies.
Geopolitical blowout: more than one
front to watch
There are three major fronts to watch in terms of geopolitical tail
risks next year. All of these would see the dollar as the big

outperformer.
A spillover of Russia’s war beyond Ukraine, or a material escalation
in cyber and hybrid attacks on European countries, would hit
European currencies the hardest. CEE currencies and the
Scandinavian bloc would be particularly exposed given their
geographical proximity and sensitivity to regional security risks.
The Swiss franc and yen would join the dollar in a big rally.
A broader Middle East conﬂict drawing in additional regional or

external powers would mainly hit FX through higher energy prices.
Net energy importers would suﬀer most, particularly currencies
where central banks have limited scope for additional tightening to
oﬀset the inﬂationary shock. Here, the dollar would be a clear
winner, as other low-yielders would suﬀer from the energy shock.
A sharp escalation in geopolitical tensions in East Asia would put
Asian currencies under heavy pressure, with AUD and NZD also

vulnerable as liquid proxies. European currencies would be relatively
more insulated from the initial shock. Regional risks could also
include US-China trade and technology tensions.
Are geopolitical risks the new normal?

重點摘要
3
• 五 中 全 會 公 報 隱 含 官 方
對 完 成 年 度 經 濟 目 標 具
相 當 信 心 ， 短 期 推 出 大
規 模 景 氣 刺 激 政 策 可 能
性降低。
• 五 中 全 會 前 後 顯 示 本 輪
政 治 整 頓 力 度 短 期 有 所
降溫， 但 整 體 水 準 仍 明
顯高於歷史均值。
《投資關鍵-反腐短暫趨緩，關注4Q景氣能否達標》
中國第三季實質GDP年增4.5%，前三季累計年增
4.6%，位居官方全年4.5%至5.0%目標區間下緣，
整體延續外強內弱格局。出口強勁，加上政策刺激
提供經濟底部支撐，然投資與消費兩大內需引擎同
步降溫，內生需求不足的結構性問題未有效改善。
展望後市，官方將擴大超長期特別國債與地方政府

專項債發行規模，加快重大基建項目落地，並持續
透過房地產去庫存、城中村改造等定向措施穩定內
需，搭配結構性寬鬆政策引導融資成本下行。惟五
中全會公報強調「確保完成全年經濟社會發展主要
目標任務」來看，官方對完成年度成長目標具相當
信心，因此第四季GDP年增率僅需維持約4.5%至
4.6%，短期推出大規模景氣刺激政策可能性降低。
本次五中全會主要聚焦黨的治理體系、監督機制與
自我革命等議題，並未釋出新一輪大規模財政或貨
幣刺激訊號。此外，觀察高盛編製的反腐敗代理指
標，五中全會前後雖略有回落，顯示本輪政治整頓
力度短期有所降溫，但整體水準仍明顯高於歷史均
值。隨著2027年新一屆黨領導層改組逐步接近，反
腐與監管力道仍有再度升溫的可能。在此背景下，
地方官員風險偏好與投資意願恐持續受到抑制，進
而削弱政策寬鬆措施向實體經濟的傳導效果，使中
國經濟仍面臨政策支持與內生需求不足並存的挑戰。
留意反貪腐力道若再度上升將壓抑政策傳導效果
資料來源：高盛，2026/10/30。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/2。

《投資關鍵-回測顯示美期中選後短線有反彈契機》
美國11/3將舉行參眾兩院的國會選舉，除視為對
當任總統的支持度檢驗之一，參眾兩院的黨派席次
也可能影響接下來政策推行速度。
回顧過去美股期中選舉期間，新興市場選前大多表
現壓抑，惟選後大多繳出正報酬佳績，上漲比率超
越五成。若以參眾兩院控制權皆翻轉的兩個年度
1994年、2006年來看，1944年因美聯準會啟動快
速升息循環，加上墨西哥金融危機爆發，導致選後
新興市場進入近一年的空頭走勢。然2006年即便
也是分裂政府，全球宏觀環境正受惠全球貿易繁榮、
商品超級周期推動經濟成長，新興市場選後12個

月累積報酬相當亮眼。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

重點摘要
5
• 同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻
轉，新興市場指數表現卻
顯著差異，意味選後更取

• 過去美股期中選舉前。台
股的平均表現最為壓抑，
但選後反彈幅度亦最為顯
著，且正報酬機率維持在

比較1994年與2006年同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻轉，
指數表現卻顯著差異，意味選後更取決於景氣與獲
利週期，而非國會席次變化本身。
進一步檢視1990年以來美國期中選舉的各市場表
現，中國上證指數受自身政策與景氣循環影響較深，
與美國期中選舉關聯性相對有限外，多數市場皆呈
現選前承壓、選後回升的特徵。其中，台股於期中
選舉前的平均表現最為壓抑，但選後反彈幅度亦最
為顯著，且正報酬機率維持在較高水準。或反應美

國政治不確定性於選後下降，市場焦點重新回歸景
氣與企業獲利基本面，帶動資金自美國逐步向Beta
值較高的市場擴散。進一步而言，若相關國家具備
較穩健的經濟基本面、企業獲利動能或產業成長題
材支撐，則更有機會在風險偏好回升與資金重新配
置的過程中，成為資金優先流入的受惠對象
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

重點摘要
6
• 美期中選對新興市場影響
較大的政策面向主要包括
關稅與貿易政策、債務上
限議題，以及政府對資料
中心建設與AI相關投資的
態度。
• 即使選後對資料中心的監
管聲浪升高，政策方向更
可能聚焦於強化治理框架
與技術管制，而非限制境
內AI投資或資料中心建設。
《投資關鍵-期中選舉後關注政策推動能力的變遷》
另一個觀察美國期中選舉後政黨版圖變化對新興市
場影響的角度，在於其對政策推動能力的改變。美
期中選對新興市場影響較大的政策面向主要包括關
稅與貿易政策、債務上限議題，以及政府對資料中
心建設與AI相關投資的態度。
首先，在關稅與貿易政策方面，由於關稅、地緣政
治及貿易談判等政策多屬行政權限範疇，政策方向
仍由白宮主導，且部分政策工具並不高度依賴國會

授權，因此政策波動性仍需關注。
其次，債務上限與財政融資需求則是影響資金流向
的重要觀察指標。國會僵局或限制積極的財政政策，
但也可能讓債務上限僵局上演，擾動市場風險偏好。
最後，近期美國各地對資料中心的反對聲浪升溫，
主要集中耗電、用水及對當地生活環境的影響。然
而，資料中心能否順利興建，關鍵仍掌握在州政府、
公共事業委員會及地方政府手中，因其負責電力、
水資源、土地分區與稅務優惠等審批事項。因此，
影響AI基建進度的主要變數未必是聯邦層級的期中
選舉，而更可能來自地方政府的政策取向。另一方
面，美國已將AI視為攸關國家競爭力、經濟成長與
國家安全的關鍵基礎設施，因此即使選後監管聲浪
升高，政策方向更可能聚焦於強化治理框架與技術
管制，而非限制境內AI投資或資料中心建設。換言
之，選後最大的差異較可能體現在建設進度與行政
程序，而非AI投資趨勢本身出現明顯逆轉。對以科
技硬體與AI供應鏈為主的新興亞洲市場而言，企業
獲利與資本支出動能仍將是比國會版圖變化更重要
的驅動因素。

重點摘要
7
• 期中選舉落幕後，焦點重
回美伊戰事若開打，油價
回升恐再度引發高利環境
的擔憂。
《投資關鍵-以長線投資視野聚焦穩健獲利標的》
展望後市，新興市場第四季仍將面臨波動升溫的環
境。 從歷史經驗與政策面觀察，美國期中選舉後
政治不確定性雖有望下降，但市場焦點最終仍將回
歸總體經濟、企業獲利與流動性環境。據傳美以軍

方擬定在美國期中選舉後再攻擊伊朗，而這可能再
度推升油價，進而引發市場對高利環境的擔憂。
基本面方面，市場預估新興市場經濟成長率將由
2026年第三季的3.4%升至第四季的4.4%，顯示整
體景氣仍具韌性。然而，高利率環境維持時間長於
預期，正考驗各國內需與投資動能；同時，已開發
國家央行偏鷹立場未見明顯鬆動，也使新興市場進
一步降息與推動寬鬆政策的空間受到壓縮。市場對
2027年新興市場景氣與企業獲利的預期差異恐將
擴大，資產表現也將更加分化。
台灣涵蓋晶圓代工、IC設計、先進封裝、AI伺服器、
PCB及網通設備等完整AI供應鏈。多層次且廣泛的
產業布局，不僅有助於分散單一產業景氣波動風險，
也使AI投資動能更全面傳導至企業獲利與出口表現，
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

在「實體人工智慧」的第二支柱下，韓
國政府的目標是將韓國打造為全球人工
智慧機器人領域的前三名。重點領域包
括：1）製造業轉型為人工智慧；2）保
障核心人工智慧技術與資料安全；3）
建立區域性大規模生產中心。在另一份
新聞稿中，韓國政府指出，將投資 20
兆韓元用於人工智慧製造業，目標是創
造 100 兆韓元的附加價值。
重點摘要
8
• 台股或因美期中選舉落幕
後重啟反彈，但美伊戰事
的開打可能再度壓抑台股
表現，預計最壞情境可留
意年線支撐，但來年第一
季有望迎更穩健表現。
進而帶動台灣在主要新興市場中持續名列獲利預期
上修幅度領先族群。
隨著市場對AI投資效益與變現能力的檢驗日趨嚴格，
具備實質獲利支撐與產業擴散效益的市場，可望更

受資金青睞，而台股憑藉完整供應鏈優勢與穩健的
獲利上修趨勢，仍具相對吸引力。本行認為台股或
因美期中選舉落幕後重啟反彈，但美伊戰事的開打
可能再度壓抑台股表現，預計最壞情境可留意年線
支撐，但來年第一季有望迎更穩健表現。
韓國股市方面， 10 月 中 旬 受 長 鑫 存儲 宣 布 將 於
2026年底推出新一代DDR5 RDIMM伺服器記憶體
影響，拖累記憶體族群股價表現。本行認為，新產
品能否形成實質供給仍有賴良率提升及客戶採用進
度驗證。然而，此事件也凸顯韓股產業結構相對集
中於記憶體與半導體領域，使指數表現較容易受到
單一產業或個別企業消息影響。疊加市場期待韓國
政府推動的資本市場改革尚未取得實質進展，也導
致市場對過去低估的韓股評價重估行情有所失望，
除外資賣壓未歇外，韓股各投資類別資金流向，9
月較大股市資金流入來自三星與海力士的企業回購，
然企業回購已告一段落。預計韓股行情從單邊上漲
轉為區間震盪，建議投資建議暫為觀望。
資料來源：MS，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10。

韓股資金行情轉淡、缺乏增量資金

重點摘要

• 中國10月景氣數據轉弱，
短期政策重心可望回歸穩
增長，但全年目標達成有
望下，預料以托底為主，
高端製造與科技發展仍是
政策主軸。
• 外需韌性與內需疲弱並存，
聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼
國產替代政策扶植的科技
族群、稀缺原物料及高附
加出口價值等產業。惟短
期科技股受全球科技板塊
回調影響，波動仍高，宜

資組合風險。
中國股市方面，儘管過去五中全會通常以審議下一
個五年規劃建議為主要任務，然由於四中全會已通
過十五五規劃，因此今年的五中全會聚焦國家安全
主軸並不意外。隨五中全會已遞補10位政治局委
員，高盛的反貪腐指數略下滑，短線政治整頓力度
降溫。中國10月官方製造業與服務業景氣領指標
再度低預期，同步跌破榮枯線，短線官方政策焦點
可能會重回穩增長，但由於達全年官方目標並不困
難，研判穩增長偏向支撐而非大力度刺激，政策傾
斜高端製造看來仍是主軸。
在整個中國經濟結構持續處外需韌性與內需疲弱的
拉鋸下，長線中國股市投資邏輯維持選股不選市，
聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技
族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。但由
於短期中國科技股同步受海外科技股回調而整理，
建議嚴控持股比重，並可適時增持有高股息分紅防
禦的金融產業來平衡波動風險。

## Document 470: セルビアは政権交代へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/セルビアは政権交代へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 27 日

MURC Focus

セルビアは政権交代へ

～揺り戻しの中で問われる対中関係のリバランス～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 総選挙で「学生リスト」が勝利 
西バルカンのセルビアで 10 月 25 日、総選挙（国民議会の一院制、定数 250 議席、比例代表制）が行われた。
即日開票の結果、先に辞任して総選挙に臨んだアレクサンダル・ヴチッチ前大統領は、自身が率いるセルビア進
歩党（SNS）を中心とする与党連合（統一セルビア）の敗北を認めた。代わって、ヴチッチ政権の汚職体質を批判
してきた学生を中心とする政治団体「学生リスト」が、国民の不満の受け皿となって勝利した（図表 1）。

この選挙は、ヴチッチ前大統領に対する、実質的な信任投票の性格を持っていた。経済面では、他国と同様
に、近年のセルビアのインフレは深刻だった。一方、中国や欧州連合（EU）からの相次いだ企業進出や人口動
態に伴う構造的な人手不足、政権による分配政策（最低賃金の引き上げなど）の影響もあって、経済は物価・賃
金スパイラルの様相を呈するに至り、国民の不満は高まっていた（図表 2）。 
図表 1. 開票結果 図表 2. セルビアのインフレと雇用

（注）開票率 96.7%時点。 
（出所）セルビア選管

（出所）セルビア統計局

〇西バルカンのセルビアで10月25日、総選挙が行われ、即日開票の結果、ヴチッチ体制への批判を展開して
きた学生を中心とする政治団体「学生リスト」が、国民の不満の受け皿となって勝利した。 
〇新政権に求められることは数多い。外交面では、中国との関係のリバランスが求められるが、セルビアと中
国の経済関係が急速に深化したことも事実であるため、現実的なスタンスが求められる。 
〇経済面では、セルビアが決められない政治に向かうことで、マクロ経済運営の“受動的な健全化”が進むと考
えられる。一方、インフラの維持管理など、能動的な財政政策運営は望みがたい。

### Page 2

政治面では、政権関係者による相次ぐ汚職などが問題視されていた。とりわけヴチッチ政権と中国との近しい
関係が、国民の強い反発を招いた側面が大きい。セルビア憲法では、大統領を務めることができる期間は通算 2
期までとされている。そのため、すでに 2 期を務めたヴチッチ前大統領は辞任のうえ、統一セルビアの首相候補
として総選挙を戦ったが、有権者による信託は得られなかった。

今後セルビアでは、大統領（アナ・ブルナビッチ国民議会議長）が新首相候補を指名し、国民議会の承認を得
たうえで新政権が発足する。現状では、学生リスト所属の議員から新首相が誕生する公算だが、同勢力には明確
な単一リーダーが存在せず、政治経験のないメンバーが数多い。したがって、セルビアは“決められない政治”に
陥る可能性が高い。

2. 問われる対中関係のリバランス 
とはいえ新政権に求められることは数多い。外交面では、中国との関係のリバランスが求められると考えられる。
ヴチッチ政権と中国との近しい関係がセルビア国民の反発を生み、今回の早期総選挙になだれ込んだ以上、新
政権が表立ってヴチッチ政権と同様の親中路線を取ることは難しい。それに、中国と緊張関係にある米国にも、
新政権は配慮しなければならない。

また EU への配慮も欠かせない。ヴチッチ政権は必ずしも EU に敵対的ではなかったが、一方で EU と緊張
関係にある中国のみならず、EU と反目するロシアからも実質的な所得移転（安価なガス供給など）を受けていた
ため、友好的でもなかった。隣国ハンガリーのように親 EU 路線への全面的な転換が果たせれば EU からの所得
移転は着実に増えるだろうが、国内には親中派や親ロ派も少なくないため、配慮が求められる。

図表 3. 対内 FDI ストックの国別内訳の推移 図表 4. 輸出入依存度の推移

（出所）セルビア国立銀行（NBS）

（出所）国際通貨基金（IMF）

### Page 3

それにこの間、セルビアと中国の経済関係が急速に深化したことも事実である。対内 FDI（外国直接投資）の
ストックを確認すると、2020 年から 2025 年の間に、中国からの FDI ストック（図表 3）は約 20 億ユーロから 70 億
ユーロと 3.5 倍も急増しており、ストック全体に占める割合も 4%から 11%まで上昇している。同様に、中国との貿
易依存度も輸入面を中心に上昇が顕著である（図表 4）。

中国企業の多くはハンガリーと国境を接するセルビア北部のヴォイヴォディナ自治州に進出し、対 EU ビジネ
スに備えている。またそうした中国企業との取引を目的に、欧州企業や日本企業も続々と進出している。こうして
形成された産業クラスターが、セルビア全体の経済成長を牽引しているため、新政権もまた中国との友好関係を
維持せざるを得ない。こうした点につき、現実的なスタンスが求められよう。

3. マクロ経済運営は“受動的な健全化”が進む 
経済面では、セルビアが決められない政治に向かうことで、マクロ経済運営の“受動的な健全化”が進むと考え
られる。そもそもセルビアの公的債務残高は、コロナショックを受けた 2021 年をピークに圧縮が進んでおり、2026
年 6 月時点で名目 GDP（国内総生産）の 45%程度にとどまっている（図表 5）。この間に進んだ物価高に伴う“イ
ンフレ税”の存在も大きいが、セルビアの債務は削減が進んでおり、相応に健全な運営がなされている。

制度上、政府による財政拡張を中銀の金融緩和で支えるようなことはできない仕組みとなっていることも、債務
の膨張を阻んでいる。具体的には、セルビア国立銀行（中央銀行、NBS）が物価目標（消費者物価の前年比上
昇率を 3%±1.5%にとどめる）に基づく金融政策運営を維持している。同時に NBS は、通貨ディナールの対ユ
ーロレートを 1 ユーロ＝117～118 ディナールで固定する厳格な通貨政策運営（管理フロート制）を堅持している。

図表 5. セルビアの公的債務残高 図表 6. セルビアの物価と金利

（注）分母の名目 GDP は 4 四半期後方移動累計値。 
（出所）セルビア財務省、同統計局

（注）インフレ目標は 3%±1.5%というレンジ。 
（出所）NBS 及びセルビア統計局

政策金利
インフレ目標

### Page 4

このように、もとから比較的健全だったセルビアのマクロ経済運営であるが、新政権の下で決められない政治に
向かうことで、良くも悪くも経済のファンダメンタルズ（基礎的条件）の変化に即した、ルールベースで機械的なマ
クロ経済運営がなされることになる。こうした意味で、セルビアのマクロ経済運営は受動的な健全化の道をたどると
予想される。

もっとも、そうした受動的な健全化は必ずしも良いことではない。グローバルな累積債務問題が懸念される中で
はあるものの、インフラの維持管理など、経済活動を営むうえで不可欠な歳出も存在する。どの国も総量的な拡
張ではなくメリハリをつけた歳出が必要となるわけだが、決められない政治の中では、そうした意味での能動的な
財政政策運営は望みがたい。

また隣国ブルガリアのように、安定政権が成立するまでに解散総選挙を短期間のうちに繰り返すような事態も
問題である。いずれにせよ、セルビア政治が安定するまでにはしばらく時間を要しよう。
